============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Immutable token representation.\n  *\n  * Case class provides:\n  * - Value equality semantics\n  * - Automatic equals/hashCode generation\n  * - Pattern matching support\n  * - Copy constructor for creating modified versions\n  *\n  * @param value The tokenized string value\n  * @param tokenType The type category of this token\n  * @param metadata Additional context about the token\n  */\ncase class Token(\n  value: String,\n  tokenType: TokenType,\n  metadata: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n) {\n  /**\n    * Return a new token with additional or updated metadata.\n    *\n    * @param additions Key-value pairs to add/update in metadata\n    * @return New Token with merged metadata\n    */\n  def withMetadata(additions: (String, Any)*): Token =\n    copy(metadata = metadata ++ additions)\n\n  /**\n    * Extract a metadata value if it exists.\n    *\n    * @param key The metadata key\n    * @return Some(value) if key exists, None otherwise\n    */\n  def getMetadata(key: String): Option[Any] = metadata.get(key)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Mutable Batch Container (Demonstrated for compatibility)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Mutable batch of tokens for scenarios requiring state mutations.\n  * Contrasts with immutable Token case class.\n  *\n  * Note: In Scala, mutable collections are typically avoided in favor of\n  * immutable ones combined with functional transformations. This class\n  * demonstrates mutable patterns when needed (e.g., incremental building).\n  */\nclass TokenBatch {\n  private var _tokens: Vector[Token] = Vector.empty\n  private var _processed: Boolean = false\n\n  def tokens: Vector[Token] = _tokens\n\n  def add(token: Token): Unit = {\n    if (_processed) throw new IllegalStateException(\"Batch already processed\")\n    _tokens = _tokens :+ token\n  }\n\n  def markProcessed(): Unit = {\n    _processed = true\n  }\n\n  def isProcessed: Boolean = _processed\n\n  def clear(): Unit = {\n    if (_processed) throw new IllegalStateException(\"Cannot clear processed batch\")\n    _tokens = Vector.empty\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Generic Container Classes (Covariance/Contravariance)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Covariant container - can return subtypes of T.\n  * Use +T to indicate covariance: if Dog extends Animal,\n  * then TokenContainer[Dog] can be used where TokenContainer[Animal] is expected.\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of items (covariant)\n  */\nclass TokenContainer[+T](items: Seq[T]) {\n  private val _items: Vector[T] = items.toVector\n\n  def getAll: Vector[T] = _items\n\n  def mapTokens(func: T => String): Vector[String] =\n    _items.map(func)\n\n  def size: Int = _items.length\n\n  def isEmpty: Boolean = _items.isEmpty\n\n  def nonEmpty: Boolean = _items.nonEmpty\n\n  def foreach(func: T => Unit): Unit = _items.foreach(func)\n}\n\n/**\n  * Contravariant sink - can accept supertypes of T.\n  * Use -T to indicate contravariance: if Animal is supertype of Dog,\n  * then TokenSink[Animal] can accept Dog values.\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of items to receive (contravariant)\n  */\nclass TokenSink[-T] {\n  private var _received: Vector[Any] = Vector.empty\n\n  def receive(item: T): Unit = {\n    _received = _received :+ item\n  }\n\n  def drain(): Vector[Any] = {\n    val result = _received\n    _received = Vector.empty\n    result\n  }\n\n  def size: Int = _received.length\n}\n\n/**\n  * Invariant handler - requires exact type matching.\n  * T without variance modifier means invariant: neither covariant nor contravariant.\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of value to handle (invariant)\n  */\nclass BivariantHandler[T](initialValue: T) {\n  private var _value: T = initialValue\n\n  def get: T = _value\n\n  def set(value: T): Unit = {\n    _value = value\n  }\n\n  def transform(func: T => T): T = {\n    _value = func(_value)\n    _value\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Base Tokenizer Trait\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Abstract trait for tokenizers with generic input type.\n  *\n  * In Scala, we use traits instead of abstract base classes for:\n  * - Better composition (can mix multiple traits)\n  * - Linearization of method resolution\n  * - Interface definition without state\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of values this tokenizer accepts\n  */\ntrait BaseTokenizer[T] {\n  /**\n    * Convert a single value to a token.\n    *\n    * @param value The value to tokenize\n    * @return A Token representing the value\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: T): Token\n\n  /**\n    * Lazily tokenize multiple values using a Stream (lazy sequence).\n    *\n    * @param values The values to tokenize\n    * @return A LazyList of tokens (evaluated on demand)\n    */\n  def tokenizeBatch(values: Iterable[T]): LazyList[Token] =\n    LazyList.from(values).map(tokenize)\n\n  /**\n    * Convert a value to a token, wrapping in Try for error handling.\n    *\n    * @param value The value to tokenize\n    * @return Try[Token] with success or failure\n    */\n  def toToken(value: T): Try[Token] = Try(tokenize(value))\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// String Tokenizer Implementation\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for string and byte array types.\n  *\n  * Handles encoding/decoding and optional normalization.\n  * Uses sealed trait to restrict T to String or Array[Byte].\n  *\n  * @param encoding Character encoding (default UTF-8)\n  * @param normalizer Optional function to normalize strings\n  */\nclass StringTokenizer(\n  encoding: String = \"UTF-8\",\n  normalizer: String => String = identity\n) extends BaseTokenizer[Either[String, Array[Byte]]] {\n\n  def tokenize(value: Either[String, Array[Byte]]): Token = {\n    val strValue = value match {\n      case Left(s) => s\n      case Right(bytes) => new String(bytes, encoding)\n    }\n\n    val normalized = normalizer(strValue)\n    Token(normalized, TokenType.String)\n  }\n}\n\nobject StringTokenizer {\n  /**\n    * Create a StringTokenizer with a normalizer that converts to lowercase.\n    */\n  def lowercaseNormalizer: StringTokenizer =\n    new StringTokenizer(normalizer = _.toLowerCase)\n\n  /**\n    * Create a StringTokenizer with a normalizer that trims whitespace.\n    */\n  def trimNormalizer: StringTokenizer =\n    new StringTokenizer(normalizer = _.trim)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Numeric Tokenizer Implementation\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for numeric types with precision handling.\n  *\n  * Uses a sealed trait to restrict T to Int, Long, Double, Float, or BigDecimal.\n  *\n  * @param precision Number of decimal places for floating point values\n  * @param formatOptions Additional formatting options\n  */\nclass NumericTokenizer(\n  precision: Int = 6,\n  formatOptions: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n) extends BaseTokenizer[Either[Either[Either[Int, Long], Either[Double, Float]], BigDecimal]] {\n\n  require(precision >= 0, \"Precision must be non-negative\")\n\n  def tokenize(value: Either[Either[Either[Int, Long], Either[Double, Float]], BigDecimal]): Token = {\n    val (strValue, originalType) = value match {\n      case Left(Left(Left(i))) =>\n        (i.toString, \"Int\")\n\n      case Left(Left(Right(l))) =>\n        (l.toString, \"Long\")\n\n      case Left(Right(Left(d))) =>\n        (String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", d.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), \"Double\")\n\n      case Left(Right(Right(f))) =>\n        (String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", f.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), \"Float\")\n\n      case Right(bd) =>\n        (String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", bd.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), \"BigDecimal\")\n    }\n\n    Token(strValue, TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> originalType))\n  }\n}\n\n// Simplified numeric tokenizer using pattern matching\nclass SimpleNumericTokenizer(precision: Int = 6) extends BaseTokenizer[Any] {\n  require(precision >= 0, \"Precision must be non-negative\")\n\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case i: Int =>\n      Token(i.toString, TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"Int\"))\n\n    case l: Long =>\n      Token(l.toString, TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"Long\"))\n\n    case d: Double =>\n      Token(f\"$d%.${precision}f\", TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"Double\"))\n\n    case f: Float =>\n      Token(f\"$f%.${precision}f\", TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"Float\"))\n\n    case bd: BigDecimal =>\n      Token(f\"$bd%.${precision}f\", TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"BigDecimal\"))\n\n    case other =>\n      throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Unsupported numeric type: ${other.getClass.getName}\")\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Temporal Tokenizer Implementation\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for date/time types.\n  *\n  * @param formatOption Optional custom format string\n  */\nclass TemporalTokenizer(formatOption: Option[String] = None) extends BaseTokenizer[Any] {\n\n  private val isoFormat = DateTimeFormatter.ISO_LOCAL_DATE_TIME\n  private val dateFormat = DateTimeFormatter.ISO_LOCAL_DATE\n\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case dt: LocalDateTime =>\n      val formatter = formatOption\n        .map(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern)\n        .getOrElse(isoFormat)\n      Token(dt.format(formatter), TokenType.Temporal)\n\n    case ld: LocalDate =>\n      val formatter = formatOption\n        .map(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern)\n        .getOrElse(dateFormat)\n      Token(ld.format(formatter), TokenType.Temporal)\n\n    case zdt: ZonedDateTime =>\n      val formatter = formatOption\n        .map(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern)\n        .getOrElse(isoFormat)\n      Token(zdt.format(formatter), TokenType.Temporal)\n\n    case other =>\n      throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Unsupported temporal type: ${other.getClass.getName}\")\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Universal Tokenizer (Polymorphic Dispatch)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer that handles multiple types with type-based dispatch.\n  *\n  * Instead of Python's duck typing, uses pattern matching for type-safe dispatch.\n  * This demonstrates Scala's preference for sealed hierarchies and exhaustive matching\n  * over runtime type checking.\n  */\nclass UniversalTokenizer {\n  private val stringTokenizer = new StringTokenizer()\n  private val numericTokenizer = new SimpleNumericTokenizer()\n  private val temporalTokenizer = new TemporalTokenizer()\n\n  /**\n    * Dispatch to appropriate tokenizer based on value type.\n    *\n    * Uses pattern matching for safe, exhaustive type handling.\n    * More idiomatic than Python's isinstance checks.\n    *\n    * @param value The value to tokenize\n    * @return Token representation of the value\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case null =>\n      Token(\"NULL\", TokenType.Null)\n\n    case s: String =>\n      stringTokenizer.tokenize(Left(s))\n\n    case b: Array[Byte] =>\n      stringTokenizer.tokenize(Right(b))\n\n    case i: Int =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(i)\n\n    case l: Long =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(l)\n\n    case d: Double =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(d)\n\n    case f: Float =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(f)\n\n    case bd: BigDecimal =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(bd)\n\n    case dt: LocalDateTime =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenize(dt)\n\n    case ld: LocalDate =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenize(ld)\n\n    case zdt: ZonedDateTime =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenize(zdt)\n\n    case t: Tokenizable =>\n      Token(t.toToken, TokenType.Structured)\n\n    case other =>\n      // Fallback: attempt string conversion with fallback marker\n      Token(other.toString, TokenType.String, Map(\"fallback\" -> true))\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Tokenizable Protocol (Structural Subtyping)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Trait representing anything that can be converted to a token string.\n  *\n  * Scala traits with single methods can be used as lightweight protocol types.\n  */\ntrait Tokenizable {\n  def toToken: String\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Token Registry with Nested Generics\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Registry with complex nested generic types and handler composition.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates proper handling of mutable state in Scala using\n  * immutable collections and functional patterns.\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of items stored in containers\n  */\nclass TokenRegistry[T] {\n  private var _registry: Map[String, TokenContainer[T]] = Map.empty\n  private var _handlers: Vector[T => Option[Token]] = Vector.empty\n\n  /**\n    * Register a container of items under a key.\n    */\n  def register(key: String, container: TokenContainer[T]): Unit = {\n    _registry = _registry.updated(key, container)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Add a handler function that attempts to tokenize an item.\n    *\n    * Handler returns Option[Token] to indicate success/failure.\n    */\n  def addHandler(handler: T => Option[Token]): Unit = {\n    _handlers = _handlers :+ handler\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Process all items in a container through all handlers.\n    *\n    * Uses first handler that returns Some(token), accumulates None for unhandled items.\n    *\n    * @param key The container key\n    * @return List of Option[Token] results\n    */\n  def process(key: String): List[Option[Token]] = {\n    _registry.get(key) match {\n      case None => List.empty\n\n      case Some(container) =>\n        container.getAll.map { item =>\n          _handlers.view\n            .map(_(item))\n            .find(_.isDefined)\n            .flatten\n        }.toList\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Functor and Monad Operations\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Generic functor wrapper for tokens with functional operations.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates functional programming patterns in Scala:\n  * - map for transformations\n  * - flatMap for chaining operations\n  * - getOrElse for null-safe extraction\n  *\n  * @tparam T The wrapped value type\n  */\ncase class TokenFunctor[T](value: T) {\n  /**\n    * Apply a function to the wrapped value.\n    */\n  def map[U](func: T => U): TokenFunctor[U] = TokenFunctor(func(value))\n\n  /**\n    * Chain operations that return TokenFunctor.\n    */\n  def flatMap[U](func: T => TokenFunctor[U]): TokenFunctor[U] = func(value)\n\n  /**\n    * Extract the value, returning a default if None.\n    */\n  def getOrElse(default: T): T = if (value == null) default else value\n}\n\n/**\n  * Monad with pure/return constructor.\n  *\n  * Extends TokenFunctor to add monadic operations.\n  */\ncase class TokenMonad[T](value: T) extends TokenFunctor[T](value) {\n  /**\n    * Applicative apply operation.\n    */\n  def ap[U](funcMonad: TokenMonad[T => U]): TokenMonad[U] =\n    TokenMonad(funcMonad.value(value))\n}\n\nobject TokenMonad {\n  /**\n    * Monad constructor (pure/return in Haskell terms).\n    */\n  def pure[T](value: T): TokenMonad[T] = TokenMonad(value)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// JSON Structure Tokenization\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for JSON structures with recursive type support.\n  *\n  * In Scala, recursive types must be explicitly defined using case classes\n  * or sealed traits, unlike Python's recursive type aliases.\n  *\n  * @param pretty Whether to format JSON with indentation\n  */\nclass JsonTokenizer(pretty: Boolean = false) {\n  import scala.util.parsing.json.JSONObject\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a JSON value.\n    *\n    * @param value The JSON value (String, Int, Boolean, Map, Seq, or null)\n    * @return Token representing the JSON\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = {\n    val jsonStr = value match {\n      case map: Map[_, _] if pretty =>\n        JSONObject(map.asInstanceOf[Map[String, Any]]).toString(2)\n      case map: Map[_, _] =>\n        JSONObject(map.asInstanceOf[Map[String, Any]]).toString()\n      case _ => value.toString\n    }\n\n    Token(jsonStr, TokenType.Structured, Map(\"json\" -> true))\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Extract and tokenize a value at a JSON path.\n    *\n    * Path format: \"key1.key2.0.key3\" (dot notation with array indices)\n    *\n    * @param value The JSON structure\n    * @param path The dot-notation path\n    * @return Some(Token) if path found, None otherwise\n    */\n  def tokenizePath(value: Any, path: String): Option[Token] = {\n    val parts = path.split(\"\\\\.\")\n    traversePath(value, parts).map(tokenize)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Traverse a JSON structure following a path.\n    *\n    * @param current Current value being traversed\n    * @param remaining Remaining path components\n    * @return Some(value) at path, None if path not found\n    */\n  private def traversePath(current: Any, remaining: List[String]): Option[Any] =\n    remaining match {\n      case Nil => Some(current)\n\n      case part :: rest =>\n        current match {\n          case map: Map[_, _] =>\n            map.asInstanceOf[Map[String, Any]]\n              .get(part)\n              .flatMap(traversePath(_, rest))\n\n          case seq: Seq[_] if part.forall(_.isDigit) =>\n            val idx = part.toInt\n            if (idx >= 0 && idx < seq.length)\n              traversePath(seq(idx), rest)\n            else\n              None\n\n          case _ => None\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Whitespace Tokenizer - Basic Text Tokenization\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer that splits text by whitespace with optional post-processing.\n  *\n  * This demonstrates practical tokenization for NLP and text processing.\n  * Uses immutable collections and functional transformations.\n  *\n  * @param lowercase Convert tokens to lowercase\n  * @param minLength Filter out tokens shorter than this\n  * @param maxLength Truncate tokens longer than this\n  * @param stripPunctuation Remove leading/trailing punctuation\n  */\nclass WhitespaceTokenizer(\n  lowercase: Boolean = false,\n  minLength: Int = 0,\n  maxLength: Option[Int] = None,\n  stripPunctuation: Boolean = false\n) {\n  require(minLength >= 0, \"minLength must be non-negative\")\n  require(maxLength.forall(_ > 0), \"maxLength must be positive\")\n\n  private val punctuation = Set(\".,!?;:'\\\"()[]{}\")\n\n  /**\n    * Process a single word into an optional token string.\n    *\n    * @param word The word to process\n    * @return Some(processed) if word is valid, None if filtered out\n    */\n  private def processToken(word: String): Option[String] = {\n    var result = word\n\n    if (stripPunctuation) {\n      result = result.filterNot(punctuation.contains(_))\n    }\n\n    if (lowercase) {\n      result = result.toLowerCase\n    }\n\n    if (result.length < minLength) {\n      None\n    } else if (maxLength.exists(result.length > _)) {\n      Some(result.take(maxLength.get))\n    } else if (result.nonEmpty) {\n      Some(result)\n    } else {\n      None\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Split text by whitespace and return list of tokens.\n    *\n    * @param text Input text to tokenize\n    * @return List of Token objects, one per word\n    */\n  def tokenize(text: String): List[Token] = {\n    val words = text.split(\"\\\\s+\").toList\n\n    words.zipWithIndex.flatMap { case (word, idx) =>\n      processToken(word).map { processed =>\n        Token(\n          value = processed,\n          tokenType = TokenType.String,\n          metadata = Map(\"position\" -> idx, \"original\" -> word)\n        )\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Split text by whitespace and return list of token strings.\n    *\n    * Convenience method that returns just the string values.\n    *\n    * @param text Input text to tokenize\n    * @return List of token strings\n    */\n  def tokenizeToStrings(text: String): List[String] =\n    tokenize(text).map(_.value)\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize text and return tokens with character positions.\n    *\n    * @param text Input text to tokenize\n    * @return List of tuples (token_string, start_pos, end_pos)\n    */\n  def tokenizeWithPositions(text: String): List[(String, Int, Int)] = {\n    val words = text.split(\"\\\\s+\").toList\n    var currentPos = 0\n    val results = scala.collection.mutable.ListBuffer[(String, Int, Int)]()\n\n    for (word <- words) {\n      val start = text.indexOf(word, currentPos)\n      val end = start + word.length\n\n      processToken(word).foreach { processed =>\n        results += ((processed, start, end))\n      }\n\n      currentPos = end\n    }\n\n    results.toList\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Return the number of tokens in the text.\n    *\n    * @param text Input text\n    * @return Token count\n    */\n  def countTokens(text: String): Int = tokenize(text).length\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Builder Pattern with Fluent Interface\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Fluent builder for creating custom tokenizers.\n  *\n  * The builder pattern with method chaining is idiomatic in Scala.\n  * Uses a sealed result type to ensure proper builder protocol.\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of values to tokenize\n  */\nclass TokenizerBuilder[T] {\n  private var _normalizers: Vector[String => String] = Vector.empty\n  private var _validators: Vector[T => Boolean] = Vector.empty\n  private var _metadata: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n\n  /**\n    * Add a normalizer function for string transformation.\n    *\n    * Normalizers are applied in the order they're added.\n    */\n  def withNormalizer(normalizer: String => String): TokenizerBuilder[T] = {\n    _normalizers = _normalizers :+ normalizer\n    this\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Add a validator function for input validation.\n    *\n    * All validators must pass for tokenization to succeed.\n    */\n  def withValidator(validator: T => Boolean): TokenizerBuilder[T] = {\n    _validators = _validators :+ validator\n    this\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Add metadata that will be attached to all tokens.\n    */\n  def withMetadata(pairs: (String, Any)*): TokenizerBuilder[T] = {\n    _metadata = _metadata ++ pairs\n    this\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Build the final tokenizer function.\n    *\n    * @return A function that tokenizes values of type T\n    */\n  def build(): T => Token = { value =>\n    // Validate\n    for (validator <- _normalizers) {\n      if (!_validators.forall(_(value))) {\n        throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Validation failed for $value\")\n      }\n    }\n\n    // Convert to string\n    var strValue = value.toString\n\n    // Normalize\n    for (normalizer <- _normalizers) {\n      strValue = normalizer(strValue)\n    }\n\n    Token(strValue, TokenType.String, _metadata)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Build with Try wrapper for error handling.\n    *\n    * @return A function that safely tokenizes values\n    */\n  def buildSafe(): T => Try[Token] = { value =>\n    Try {\n      // Validate\n      if (!_validators.forall(_(value))) {\n        throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Validation failed for $value\")\n      }\n\n      // Convert to string\n      var strValue = value.toString\n\n      // Normalize\n      for (normalizer <- _normalizers) {\n        strValue = normalizer(strValue)\n      }\n\n      Token(strValue, TokenType.String, _metadata)\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nobject TokenizerBuilder {\n  /**\n    * Create a new builder for the given type.\n    */\n  def apply[T](): TokenizerBuilder[T] = new TokenizerBuilder[T]()\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Convenience Functions (Top-level)\n// ============================================================================\n\nobject Tokenizer {\n  /**\n    * Convenience function to tokenize a single value using UniversalTokenizer.\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = new UniversalTokenizer().tokenize(value)\n\n  /**\n    * Convenience function to tokenize a batch of values.\n    *\n    * @param Implementation\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for date/time types.\n  *\n  * @param formatOption Optional custom format string\n  */\nclass TemporalTokenizer(formatOption: Option[String] = None) extends BaseTokenizer[Any] {\n\n  private val isoFormat = DateTimeFormatter.ISO_LOCAL_DATE_TIME\n  private val dateFormat = DateTimeFormatter.ISO_LOCAL_DATE\n\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case dt: LocalDateTime =>\n      val formatter = formatOption\n        .map(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern)\n ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer that handles multiple types with type-based dispatch.\n  *\n  * Instead of Python's duck typing, uses private val stringTokenizer = new StringTokenizer()\n  private val numericTokenizer = new SimpleNumericTokenizer()\n  private val temporalTokenizer = new TemporalTokenizer()\n\n  /**\n    * Dispatch (Structural Subtyping)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Trait representing anything that can be converted to a token string.\n  *\n  * Scala ============================================================================\n// Token Registry with Nested Generics\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Registry with complex nested generic types and handler composition.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates proper handling of mutable state in Scala using\n  * immutable collections and functional patterns.\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of items stored in containers\n  */\nclass TokenRegistry[T] {\n  private var _registry: Map[String, TokenContainer[T]] = Map.empty\n  private var _handlers: Vector[T => Option[Token]] = Vector.empty\n\n  /**\n    * Register a container of items under a key.\n    */\n  def register(key: container.getAll.map { item =>\n          _handlers.view\n            .map(_(item))\n            .find(_.isDefined)\n            .flatten\n        }.toList\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Functor and Monad Operations\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Generic functor wrapper for tokens with functional operations.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates functional programming patterns in Scala:\n  * - map for transformations\n  * - flatMap for chaining operations\n  * - getOrElse for null-safe extraction\n  *\n  * @tparam T The wrapped value type\n  */\ncase class TokenFunctor[T](value: T) {\n  /**\n    * Apply a function to the wrapped value.\n    */\n  def map[U](func: T => U): TokenFunctor[U] = TokenFunctor(func(value))\n\n  /**\n    * Chain operations that return TokenFunctor.\n    */\n  def flatMap[U](func: T => TokenFunctor[U]): Tokenization\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for JSON structures with recursive type support.\n  *\n  * In Scala, recursive types must be explicitly defined using case classes\n  * or sealed traits, unlike Python's recursive type aliases.\n  *\n  * @param pretty Whether to format JSON with indentation\n  */\nclass JsonTokenizer(pretty: Boolean = false) {\n  import scala.util.parsing.json.JSONObject\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a JSON value.\n    *\n    * @param value The JSON value (String, Int, Boolean, Map, Seq,  Token(jsonStr, TokenType.Structured, Map(\"json\" -> true))\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Extract and tokenize a value at a JSON path.\n    *\n    * Path format: \"key1.key2.0.key3\" (dot notation with array indices)\n    *\n    * @param value The JSON structure\n    * @param path The dot-notation path\n    * @return Some(Token) if path found, None otherwise\n    */\n  def tokenizePath(value: Any, path: String): Option[Token] = {\n    val parts = path.split(\"\\\\.\")\n    traversePath(value, parts).map(tokenize)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Traverse a JSON structure following a path.\n    *\n    * @param current Current value being traversed\n    * @param remaining Remaining path components\n    * @return Some(value) at path, None if path not found\n    */\n  private def traversePath(current: Any, remaining: List[String]): Option[Any] =\n    remaining match {\n      case Nil => Some(current)\n\n      case part :: rest =>\n        current match {\n          case map: Map[_, _] =>\n            map.asInstanceOf[Map[String, Any]]\n              .get(part)\n              .flatMap(traversePath(_, rest))\n\n          case seq: Seq[_] if part.forall(_.isDigit) =>\n            val idx = part.toInt\n            if (idx >= 0 && idx < seq.length)\n              traversePath(seq(idx), rest)\n            else\n              None\n\n          case _ => None\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Whitespace Tokenizer - Basic Text Tokenization\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer that splits text by whitespace with optional post-processing.\n  *\n  * This demonstrates practical tokenization for NLP and text processing.\n  * Uses immutable collections and functional transformations.\n  *\n  * @param lowercase Convert tokens to lowercase\n  * @param minLength Filter out tokens shorter than this\n  * @param maxLength Truncate tokens longer than this\n  * @param stripPunctuation Remove leading/trailing punctuation\n  */\nclass WhitespaceTokenizer(\n  lowercase: Boolean = false,\n  minLength: Int = 0,\n  maxLength: Option[Int] = None,\n  stripPunctuation: Boolean = false\n) {\n  require(minLength >= 0, \"minLength must be non-negative\")\n  require(maxLength.forall(_ > 0), \"maxLength must be positive\")\n\n  private val punctuation = Set(\".,!?;:'\\\"()[]{}\")\n\n  /**\n    * Process a single word into an optional token string.\n    *\n    * @param word The word to process\n    * @return Some(processed) if word is valid, None if filtered out\n    */\n  private def processToken(word: String): Option[String] = {\n    var result = word\n\n    if (stripPunctuation) {\n      result = result.filterNot(punctuation.contains(_))\n    }\n\n    if (lowercase) {\n      result = result.toLowerCase\n    }\n\n    if (result.length < minLength) {\n      None\n    } else if (maxLength.exists(result.length > _)) {\n      Some(result.take(maxLength.get))\n    } else if (result.nonEmpty) {\n      Some(result)\n    } else {\n      None\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Split text by whitespace and return list of tokens.\n    *\n    * @param text Input text to tokenize\n    * @return List of Token objects, one per word\n    */\n  def tokenize(text: String): List[Token] = {\n tokenType = TokenType.String,\n          metadata = Map(\"position\" -> idx, \"original\" -> word)\n        )\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Split text by whitespace and return list of token standardized tokens.\n  *\n  * This Scala implementation translates the Python tokenizer module with\n  * idiomatic Scala patterns including sealed hierarchies, case classes,\n  * Option handling, and proper immutability.\n  *\n  * Key design decisions:\n  * - TokenType as a sealed trait instead of Enum for better extensibility\n  * - Token as a case class for immutability and automatic equals/hashCode\n  * - BaseTokenizer as a trait with default implementations\n  * - Option[T] instead of null returns\n  * - Pattern matching instead of isinstance checks\n  * - Proper error handling Immutable token representation.\n  *\n  * Case class provides:\n  * - Value equality semantics\n  * - Automatic equals/hashCode generation\n  * - Pattern matching support\n  * - Copy constructor for creating modified versions\n  *\n  * @param value The tokenized string value\n  * @param tokenType The type category of this token\n  * @param metadata Additional context about the token\n  */\ncase class Token(\n  value: String,\n  tokenType: TokenType,\n  metadata: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n) {\n  /**\n    * Return a new token with additional or updated metadata.\n    *\n    * @param additions Key-value pairs to add/update in metadata\n    * @return New Token with merged metadata\n    */\n  def withMetadata(additions: (String, Any)*): Token =\n    copy(metadata = metadata ++ additions)\n\n  /**\n    * Extract a metadata value if it exists.\n    *\n    * @param key The metadata key\n    * @return Some(value) if key exists, None otherwise\n    */\n  def getMetadata(key: String): Option[Any] = metadata.get(key)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Mutable Batch Container (Demonstrated for compatibility)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Mutable batch of tokens for scenarios requiring state mutations.\n  * Contrasts with immutable Token case class.\n  *\n  * Note: In Scala, mutable collections are typically avoided in favor of\n  * immutable ones combined with functional transformations. This class\n  * demonstrates mutable patterns when needed (e.g., incremental building).\n  */\nclass TokenBatch {\n  private var _tokens: Vector[Token] = Vector.empty\n  private var _processed: Boolean = false\n\n  def tokens: Vector[Token] = _tokens\n\n  def add(token: Token): Unit = {\n    if (_processed) throw new IllegalStateException(\"Batch already processed\")\n    _tokens = _tokens :+ token\n  }\n\n  def markProcessed(): Unit = {\n    _processed = true\n  }\n\n  def isProcessed: Boolean = _processed\n\n  def clear(): Unit = {\n    if (_processed) throw new IllegalStateException(\"Cannot clear processed batch\")\n    _tokens = Vector.empty\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Generic Container Classes (Covariance/Contravariance)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Covariant container - can return subtypes of T.\n  * Use +T to indicate covariance: if Dog extends Animal,\n  * then TokenContainer[Dog] can be used where TokenContainer[Animal] is expected.\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of items (covariant)\n  */\nclass TokenContainer[+T](items: Seq[T]) {\n  private val _items: Vector[T] = items.toVector\n\n  def getAll: Vector[T] = _items\n\n  def mapTokens(func: T => String): Vector[String] =\n    _items.map(func)\n\n  def size: Int = _items.length\n\n  def isEmpty: Boolean = _items.isEmpty\n\n  def nonEmpty: Boolean = _items.nonEmpty\n\n  def foreach(func: T => Unit): Unit = _items.foreach(func)\n}\n\n/**\n  * Contravariant sink - can accept supertypes of T.\n  * Use -T to indicate contravariance: if Animal is supertype of Dog,\n  * then TokenSink[Animal] can accept Dog values.\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of items to receive (contravariant)\n  */\nclass TokenSink[-T] {\n  private var _received: Vector[Any] = Vector.empty\n\n  def receive(item: T): Unit = {\n    _received = _received :+ item\n  }\n\n  def drain(): Vector[Any] = {\n    val result = _received\n    _received = Vector.empty\n    result\n  }\n\n  def size: Int = _received.length\n}\n\n/**\n  * Invariant handler - requires exact type matching.\n  * T without variance modifier means invariant: neither covariant nor contravariant.\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of value to handle (invariant)\n  */\nclass BivariantHandler[T](initialValue: T) {\n  private var _value: T = initialValue\n\n  def get: T = _value\n\n  def set(value: T): Unit = {\n    _value = value\n  }\n\n  def transform(func: T => T): T = {\n    _value = func(_value)\n    _value\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Base Tokenizer Trait\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Abstract trait for tokenizers with generic input type.\n  *\n  * In Scala, we use traits instead of abstract base classes for:\n  * - Better composition (can mix multiple traits)\n  * - Linearization of method resolution\n  * - Interface definition without state\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of values this tokenizer accepts\n  */\ntrait BaseTokenizer[T] {\n  /**\n    * Convert a single value to a token.\n    *\n    * @param value The value to tokenize\n    * @return A Token representing the value\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: T): Token\n\n  /**\n    * Lazily tokenize multiple values using a Stream (lazy sequence).\n    *\n    * @param values The values to tokenize\n    * @return A LazyList of tokens (evaluated on demand)\n    */\n  def tokenizeBatch(values: Iterable[T]): LazyList[Token] =\n    LazyList.from(values).map(tokenize)\n\n  /**\n    * Convert a value to a token, wrapping in Try for error handling.\n    *\n    * @param value The value to tokenize\n    * @return Try[Token] with success or failure\n    */\n  def toToken(value: T): Try[Token] = Try(tokenize(value))\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// String Tokenizer Implementation\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for string and byte array types.\n  *\n  * Handles encoding/decoding and optional normalization.\n  * Uses sealed trait to restrict T to String or Array[Byte].\n  *\n  * @param encoding Character encoding (default UTF-8)\n  * @param normalizer Optional function to normalize strings\n  */\nclass StringTokenizer(\n  encoding: String = \"UTF-8\",\n  normalizer: String => String = identity\n) extends BaseTokenizer[Either[String, Array[Byte]]] {\n\n  def tokenize(value: Either[String, Array[Byte]]): Token = {\n    val strValue = value match {\n      case Left(s) => s\n      case Right(bytes) => new String(bytes, encoding)\n    }\n\n    val normalized = normalizer(strValue)\n    Token(normalized, TokenType.String)\n  }\n}\n\nobject StringTokenizer {\n  /**\n    * Create a StringTokenizer with a normalizer that converts to lowercase.\n    */\n  def lowercaseNormalizer: StringTokenizer =\n    new StringTokenizer(normalizer = _.toLowerCase)\n\n  /**\n    * Create a StringTokenizer with a normalizer that trims whitespace.\n    */\n  def trimNormalizer: StringTokenizer =\n    new StringTokenizer(normalizer = _.trim)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Numeric Tokenizer Implementation\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for numeric types with precision handling.\n  *\n  * Uses a sealed trait to restrict T to Int, Long, Double, Float, or BigDecimal.\n  *\n  * @param precision Number of decimal places for floating point values\n  * @param formatOptions Additional formatting options\n  */\nclass NumericTokenizer(\n  precision: Int = 6,\n  formatOptions: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n) extends BaseTokenizer[Either[Either[Either[Int, Long], Either[Double, Float]], BigDecimal]] {\n\n  require(precision >= 0, \"Precision must be non-negative\")\n\n  def tokenize(value: Either[Either[Either[Int, Long], Either[Double, Float]], BigDecimal]): Token = {\n    val (strValue, originalType) = value match {\n      case Left(Left(Left(i))) =>\n        (i.toString, \"Int\")\n\n      case Left(Left(Right(l))) =>\n        (l.toString, \"Long\")\n\n      case Left(Right(Left(d))) =>\n        (String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", d.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), \"Double\")\n\n      case Left(Right(Right(f))) =>\n        (String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", f.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), \"Float\")\n\n      case Right(bd) =>\n        (String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", bd.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), \"BigDecimal\")\n    }\n\n    Token(strValue, TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> originalType))\n  }\n}\n\n// Simplified numeric tokenizer using pattern matching\nclass SimpleNumericTokenizer(precision: Int = 6) extends BaseTokenizer[Any] {\n  require(precision >= 0, \"Precision must be non-negative\")\n\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case i: Int =>\n      Token(i.toString, TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"Int\"))\n\n    case l: Long =>\n      Token(l.toString, TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"Long\"))\n\n    case d: Double =>\n      Token(String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", d.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"Double\"))\n\n    case f: Float =>\n      Token(String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", f.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"Float\"))\n\n    case bd: BigDecimal =>\n      Token(String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", bd.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"BigDecimal\"))\n\n    case other =>\n      throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Unsupported numeric type: ${other.getClass.getName}\")\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Temporal Tokenizer Implementation\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for date/time types.\n  *\n  * @param formatOption Optional custom format string\n  */\nclass TemporalTokenizer(formatOption: Option[String] = None) extends BaseTokenizer[Any] {\n\n  private val isoFormat = DateTimeFormatter.ISO_LOCAL_DATE_TIME\n  private val dateFormat = DateTimeFormatter.ISO_LOCAL_DATE\n\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case dt: LocalDateTime =>\n      val formatter = formatOption\n        .map(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern)\n        .getOrElse(isoFormat)\n      Token(dt.format(formatter), TokenType.Temporal)\n\n    case ld: LocalDate =>\n      val formatter = formatOption\n        .map(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern)\n        .getOrElse(dateFormat)\n      Token(ld.format(formatter), TokenType.Temporal)\n\n    case zdt: ZonedDateTime =>\n      val formatter = formatOption\n        .map(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern)\n        .getOrElse(isoFormat)\n      Token(zdt.format(formatter), TokenType.Temporal)\n\n    case other =>\n      throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Unsupported temporal type: ${other.getClass.getName}\")\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Universal Tokenizer (Polymorphic Dispatch)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer that handles multiple types with type-based dispatch.\n  *\n  * Instead of Python's duck typing, uses pattern matching for type-safe dispatch.\n  * This demonstrates Scala's preference for sealed hierarchies and exhaustive matching\n  * over runtime type checking.\n  */\nclass UniversalTokenizer {\n  private val stringTokenizer = new StringTokenizer()\n  private val numericTokenizer = new SimpleNumericTokenizer()\n  private val temporalTokenizer = new TemporalTokenizer()\n\n  /**\n    * Dispatch to appropriate tokenizer based on value type.\n    *\n    * Uses pattern matching for safe, exhaustive type handling.\n    * More idiomatic than Python's isinstance checks.\n    *\n    * @param value The value to tokenize\n    * @return Token representation of the value\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case null =>\n      Token(\"NULL\", TokenType.Null)\n\n    case s: String =>\n      stringTokenizer.tokenize(Left(s))\n\n    case b: Array[Byte] =>\n      stringTokenizer.tokenize(Right(b))\n\n    case i: Int =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(i)\n\n    case l: Long =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(l)\n\n    case d: Double =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(d)\n\n    case f: Float =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(f)\n\n    case bd: BigDecimal =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(bd)\n\n    case dt: LocalDateTime =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenize(dt)\n\n    case ld: LocalDate =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenize(ld)\n\n    case zdt: ZonedDateTime =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenize(zdt)\n\n    case t: Tokenizable =>\n      Token(t.toToken, TokenType.Structured)\n\n    case other =>\n      // Fallback: attempt string conversion with fallback marker\n      Token(other.toString, TokenType.String, Map(\"fallback\" -> true))\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Tokenizable Protocol (Structural Subtyping)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Trait representing anything that can be converted to a token string.\n  *\n  * Scala traits with single methods can be used as lightweight protocol types.\n  */\ntrait Tokenizable {\n  def toToken: String\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Token Registry with Nested Generics\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Registry with complex nested generic types and handler composition.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates proper handling of mutable state in Scala using\n  * immutable collections and functional patterns.\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of items stored in containers\n  */\nclass TokenRegistry[T] {\n  private var _registry: Map[String, TokenContainer[T]] = Map.empty\n  private var _handlers: Vector[T => Option[Token]] = Vector.empty\n\n  /**\n    * Register a container of items under a key.\n    */\n  def register(key: String, container: TokenContainer[T]): Unit = {\n    _registry = _registry.updated(key, container)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Add a handler function that attempts to tokenize an item.\n    *\n    * Handler returns Option[Token] to indicate success/failure.\n    */\n  def addHandler(handler: T => Option[Token]): Unit = {\n    _handlers = _handlers :+ handler\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Process all items in a container through all handlers.\n    *\n    * Uses first handler that returns Some(token), accumulates None for unhandled items.\n    *\n    * @param key The container key\n    * @return List of Option[Token] results\n    */\n  def process(key: String): List[Option[Token]] = {\n    _registry.get(key) match {\n      case None => List.empty\n\n      case Some(container) =>\n        container.getAll.map { item =>\n          _handlers.view\n            .map(_(item))\n            .find(_.isDefined)\n            .flatten\n        }.toList\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Functor and Monad Operations\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Generic functor wrapper for tokens with functional operations.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates functional programming patterns in Scala:\n  * - map for transformations\n  * - flatMap for chaining operations\n  * - getOrElse for null-safe extraction\n  *\n  * @tparam T The wrapped value type\n  */\ncase class TokenFunctor[T](value: T) {\n  /**\n    * Apply a function to the wrapped value.\n    */\n  def map[U](func: T => U): TokenFunctor[U] = TokenFunctor(func(value))\n\n  /**\n    * Chain operations that return TokenFunctor.\n    */\n  def flatMap[U](func: T => TokenFunctor[U]): TokenFunctor[U] = func(value)\n\n  /**\n    * Extract the value, returning a default if None.\n    */\n  def getOrElse(default: T): T = if (value == null) default else value\n}\n\n/**\n  * Monad with pure/return constructor.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates monadic operations without case class inheritance\n  * (which is not allowed in Scala 2.13).\n  */\nclass TokenMonad[T](val value: T) extends TokenFunctor[T](value) {\n  /**\n    * Applicative apply operation.\n    */\n  def ap[U](funcMonad: TokenMonad[T => U]): TokenMonad[U] =\n    new TokenMonad(funcMonad.value(value))\n\n  override def equals(obj: Any): Boolean = obj match {\n    case other: TokenMonad[_] => value == other.value\n    case _ => false\n  }\n\n  override def hashCode(): Int = value.hashCode()\n\n  override def toString: String = s\"TokenMonad($value)\"\n}\n\nobject TokenMonad {\n  /**\n    * Monad constructor (pure/return in Haskell terms).\n    */\n  def pure[T](value: T): TokenMonad[T] = TokenMonad(value)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// JSON Structure Tokenization\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for JSON structures with recursive type support.\n  *\n  * In Scala, recursive types must be explicitly defined using case classes\n  * or sealed traits, unlike Python's recursive type aliases.\n  *\n  * @param pretty Whether to format JSON with indentation\n  */\nclass JsonTokenizer(pretty: Boolean = false) {\n  import scala.util.parsing.json.JSONObject\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a JSON value.\n    *\n    * @param value The JSON value (String, Int, Boolean, Map, Seq, or null)\n    * @return Token representing the JSON\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = {\n    val jsonStr = value match {\n      case map: Map[_, _] if pretty =>\n        JSONObject(map.asInstanceOf[Map[String, Any]]).toString(2)\n      case map: Map[_, _] =>\n        JSONObject(map.asInstanceOf[Map[String, Any]]).toString()\n      case _ => value.toString\n    }\n\n    Token(jsonStr, TokenType.Structured, Map(\"json\" -> true))\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Extract and tokenize a value at a JSON path.\n    *\n    * Path format: \"key1.key2.0.key3\" (dot notation with array indices)\n    *\n    * @param value The JSON structure\n    * @param path The dot-notation path\n    * @return Some(Token) if path found, None otherwise\n    */\n  def tokenizePath(value: Any, path: String): Option[Token] = {\n    val parts = path.split(\"\\\\.\")\n    traversePath(value, parts).map(tokenize)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Traverse a JSON structure following a path.\n    *\n    * @param current Current value being traversed\n    * @param remaining Remaining path components\n    * @return Some(value) at path, None if path not found\n    */\n  private def traversePath(current: Any, remaining: List[String]): Option[Any] =\n    remaining match {\n      case Nil => Some(current)\n\n      case part :: rest =>\n        current match {\n          case map: Map[_, _] =>\n            map.asInstanceOf[Map[String, Any]]\n              .get(part)\n              .flatMap(traversePath(_, rest))\n\n          case seq: Seq[_] if part.forall(_.isDigit) =>\n            val idx = part.toInt\n            if (idx >= 0 && idx < seq.length)\n              traversePath(seq(idx), rest)\n            else\n              None\n\n          case _ => None\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Whitespace Tokenizer - Basic Text Tokenization\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer that splits text by whitespace with optional post-processing.\n  *\n  * This demonstrates practical tokenization for NLP and text processing.\n  * Uses immutable collections and functional transformations.\n  *\n  * @param lowercase Convert tokens to lowercase\n  * @param minLength Filter out tokens shorter than this\n  * @param maxLength Truncate tokens longer than this\n  * @param stripPunctuation Remove leading/trailing punctuation\n  */\nclass WhitespaceTokenizer(\n  lowercase: Boolean = false,\n  minLength: Int = 0,\n  maxLength: Option[Int] = None,\n  stripPunctuation: Boolean = false\n) {\n  require(minLength >= 0, \"minLength must be non-negative\")\n  require(maxLength.forall(_ > 0), \"maxLength must be positive\")\n\n  private val punctuation = Set(\".,!?;:'\\\"()[]{}\")\n\n  /**\n    * Process a single word into an optional token string.\n    *\n    * @param word The word to process\n    * @return Some(processed) if word is valid, None if filtered out\n    */\n  private def processToken(word: String): Option[String] = {\n    var result = word\n\n    if (stripPunctuation) {\n      result = result.filterNot(punctuation.contains(_))\n    }\n\n    if (lowercase) {\n      result = result.toLowerCase\n    }\n\n    if (result.length < minLength) {\n      None\n    } else if (maxLength.exists(result.length > _)) {\n      Some(result.take(maxLength.get))\n    } else if (result.nonEmpty) {\n      Some(result)\n    } else {\n      None\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Split text by whitespace and return list of tokens.\n    *\n    * @param text Input text to tokenize\n    * @return List of Token objects, one per word\n    */\n  def tokenize(text: String): List[Token] = {\n    val words = text.split(\"\\\\s+\").toList\n\n    words.zipWithIndex.flatMap { case (word, idx) =>\n      processToken(word).map { processed =>\n        Token(\n          value = processed,\n          tokenType = TokenType.String,\n          metadata = Map(\"position\" -> idx, \"original\" -> word)\n        )\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Split text by whitespace and return list of token strings.\n    *\n    * Convenience method that returns just the string values.\n    *\n    * @param text Input text to tokenize\n    * @return List of token strings\n    */\n  def tokenizeToStrings(text: String): List[String] =\n    tokenize(text).map(_.value)\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize text and return tokens with character positions.\n    *\n    * @param text Input text to tokenize\n    * @return List of tuples (token_string, start_pos, end_pos)\n    */\n  def tokenizeWithPositions(text: String): List[(String, Int, Int)] = {\n    val words = text.split(\"\\\\s+\").toList\n    var currentPos = 0\n    val results = scala.collection.mutable.ListBuffer[(String, Int, Int)]()\n\n    for (word <- words) {\n      val start = text.indexOf(word, currentPos)\n      val end = start + word.length\n\n      processToken(word).foreach { processed =>\n        results += ((processed, start, end))\n      }\n\n      currentPos = end\n    }\n\n    results.toList\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Return the number of tokens in the text.\n    *\n    * @param text Input text\n    * @return Token count\n    */\n  def countTokens(text: String): Int = tokenize(text).length\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Builder Pattern with Fluent Interface\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Fluent builder for creating custom tokenizers.\n  *\n  * The builder pattern with method chaining is idiomatic in Scala.\n  * Uses a sealed result type to ensure proper builder protocol.\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of values to tokenize\n  */\nclass TokenizerBuilder[T] {\n  private var _normalizers: Vector[String => String] = Vector.empty\n  private var _validators: Vector[T => Boolean] = Vector.empty\n  private var _metadata: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n\n  /**\n    * Add a normalizer function for string transformation.\n    *\n    * Normalizers are applied in the order they're added.\n    */\n  def withNormalizer(normalizer: String => String): TokenizerBuilder[T] = {\n    _normalizers = _normalizers :+ normalizer\n    this\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Add a validator function for input validation.\n    *\n    * All validators must pass for tokenization to succeed.\n    */\n  def withValidator(validator: T => Boolean): TokenizerBuilder[T] = {\n    _validators = _validators :+ validator\n    this\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Add metadata that will be attached to all tokens.\n    */\n  def withMetadata(pairs: (String, Any)*): TokenizerBuilder[T] = {\n    _metadata = _metadata ++ pairs\n    this\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Build the final tokenizer function.\n    *\n    * @return A function that tokenizes values of type T\n    */\n  def build(): T => Token = { value =>\n    // Validate\n    for (validator <- _normalizers) {\n      if (!_validators.forall(_(value))) {\n        throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Validation failed for $value\")\n      }\n    }\n\n    // Convert to string\n    var strValue = value.toString\n\n    // Normalize\n    for (normalizer <- _normalizers) {\n      strValue = normalizer(strValue)\n    }\n\n    Token(strValue, TokenType.String, _metadata)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Build with Try wrapper for error handling.\n    *\n    * @return A function that safely tokenizes values\n    */\n  def buildSafe(): T => Try[Token] = { value =>\n    Try {\n      // Validate\n      if (!_validators.forall(_(value))) {\n        throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Validation failed for $value\")\n      }\n\n      // Convert to string\n      var strValue = value.toString\n\n      // Normalize\n      for (normalizer <- _normalizers) {\n        strValue = normalizer(strValue)\n      }\n\n      Token(strValue, TokenType.String, _metadata)\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nobject TokenizerBuilder {\n  /**\n    * Create a new builder for the given type.\n    */\n  def apply[T](): TokenizerBuilder[T] = new TokenizerBuilder[T]()\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Convenience Functions (Top-level)\n// ============================================================================\n\nobject Tokenizer {\n  /**\n    * Convenience function to tokenize a single value using UniversalTokenizer.\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = new UniversalTokenizer().tokenize(value)\n\n  /**\n    * Convenience function to tokenize a batch of values.\n    *\n    * @param values Values to tokenize\n    * @return LazyList of tokens (evaluated on demand)\n    */\n  def tokenizeBatch(values: Iterable[Any]): LazyList[Token] =\n    LazyList.from(values).map(tokenize)\n\n  /**\n    * Convenience function to safely tokenize a value with error handling.\n    */\n  def toToken(value: Any): Try[Token] = Try(tokenize(value))\n\n  /**\n    * Convenience function to tokenize a value with attached metadata.\n    */\n  def withMetadata(value: Any, metadata: (String, Any)*): Token =\n    tokenize(value).withMetadata(metadata: }\n}\n\n// Simplified numeric tokenizer using pattern matching\nclass SimpleNumericTokenizer(precision: Int = 6) extends BaseTokenizer[Any] {\n  require(precision >= 0, \"Precision must be  */\nclass TemporalTokenizer(formatOption: Option[String] = None) extends BaseTokenizer[Any] {\n\n  private val isoFormat = DateTimeFormatter.ISO_LOCAL_DATE_TIME\n  private val dateFormat = DateTimeFormatter.ISO_LOCAL_DATE\n\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case dt: LocalDateTime =>\n      val formatter = formatOption\n        .map(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern)\n ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer that handles multiple types with type-based dispatch.\n  *\n  * Instead of Python's duck typing, uses private val stringTokenizer = new StringTokenizer()\n  private val numericTokenizer = new SimpleNumericTokenizer()\n  private val temporalTokenizer = new TemporalTokenizer()\n\n  /**\n    * Dispatch to appropriate tokenizer based on value type.\n    *\n    * Uses pattern matching for safe, exhaustive type handling.\n    * More idiomatic than Python's isinstance checks.\n    *\n    * @param value s: String =>\n      stringTokenizer.tokenize(Left(s))\n\n    case b: Array[Byte] =>\n      stringTokenizer.tokenize(Right(b))\n\n    case i: Int =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(i)\n\n    case l: (Structural Subtyping)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Trait representing anything that can be converted to a token string.\n  *\n  * Scala ============================================================================\n// Token Registry with Nested Generics\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Registry with complex nested generic types and handler composition.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates proper handling of mutable state in Scala using\n  * immutable collections and functional patterns.\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of items stored in containers\n  */\nclass TokenRegistry[T] {\n  private var _registry: Map[String, TokenContainer[T]] = Map.empty\n  private var _handlers: Vector[T => Option[Token]] = Vector.empty\n\n  /**\n    * Register a container of items under a key.\n    */\n  def register(key: String, container: TokenContainer[T]): Unit = {\n    _registry = _registry.updated(key, container)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Add a handler function that attempts to tokenize an item.\n    *\n    * Handler returns Option[Token] to indicate success/failure.\n    */\n  def addHandler(handler: T => Option[Token]): Unit = {\n    _handlers = _handlers :+ handler\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Process all items in a container through all handlers.\n    *\n    * Uses first handler that returns Some(token), accumulates None for unhandled items.\n    *\n    * @param key The container key\n    * @return List of Option[Token] results\n    */\n  def process(key: String): List[Option[Token]] = {\n    _registry.get(key) match {\n      case None => List.empty\n\n      case Some(container) =>\n        container.getAll.map { item =>\n          _handlers.view\n            .map(_(item))\n            .find(_.isDefined)\n            .flatten\n        }.toList\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Functor and Monad Operations\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Generic functor wrapper for tokens with functional operations.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates functional programming patterns in Scala:\n  * - map for transformations\n  * - flatMap for chaining operations\n  * - getOrElse for null-safe extraction\n  *\n  * @tparam T The wrapped value type\n  */\ncase class TokenFunctor[T](value: T) {\n  /**\n    * Apply a function to the wrapped value.\n    */\n  def map[U](func: T => U): TokenFunctor[U] = TokenFunctor(func(value))\n\n  /**\n    * Chain operations that return TokenFunctor.\n    */\n  def flatMap[U](func: T => TokenFunctor[U]): TokenFunctor[U] = func(value)\n\n  /**\n    * Extract the value, returning a default if None.\n    */\n  def getOrElse(default: T): T = if (value == null) default else value\n}\n\n/**\n  * Monad with pure/return constructor.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates monadic operations without case class inheritance\n  * (which is not allowed in Scala 2.13).\n  */\nclass TokenMonad[T](val value: T) extends TokenFunctor[T](value) {\n  /**\n    * Applicative apply operation.\n    */\n  def ap[U](funcMonad: TokenMonad[T => U]): TokenMonad[U] =\n    new TokenMonad(funcMonad.value(value))\n\n  override def equals(obj: Any): Boolean = obj match {\n    case other: TokenMonad[_] => value == other.value\n    case _ => false\n  }\n\n  override def hashCode(): Int = value.hashCode()\n\n  override def toString: String = s\"TokenMonad($value)\"\n}\n\nobject TokenMonad {\n  /**\n    * Monad constructor (pure/return in Haskell terms).\n    */\n  def pure[T](value: T): TokenMonad[T] = TokenMonad(value)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// JSON Structure Tokenization\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for JSON structures with recursive type support.\n  *\n  * In Scala, recursive types must be explicitly defined using case classes\n  * or sealed traits, unlike Python's recursive type aliases.\n  *\n  * Uses simple JSON serialization without external dependencies.\n  *\n  * @param pretty Whether to format JSON with indentation\n  */\nclass JsonTokenizer(pretty: Boolean = false) {\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a JSON value.\n    *\n    * @param value The JSON value (String, Int, Boolean, Map, Seq, or null)\n    * @return Token representing the JSON\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = {\n    val jsonStr = serializeToJson(value, 0)\n    Token(jsonStr, TokenType.Structured, Map(\"json\" -> true))\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Simple JSON serialization helper.\n    */\n  private def serializeToJson(value: Any, depth: Int): String = {\n    val indent = if (pretty) \"  \" * depth else \"\"\n    val nextIndent = if (pretty) \"  \" * (depth + 1) else \"\"\n    val newline = if (pretty) \"\\n\" else \"\"\n\n    value match {\n      case null => \"null\"\n      case b: Boolean => b.toString\n      case n: Number => n.toString\n      case s: String => \"\\\"\" + escapeString(s) + \"\\\"\"\n      case map: Map[_, _] =>\n        if (map.isEmpty) \"{}\"\n        else {\n          val pairs = map.asInstanceOf[Map[String, Any]].map { case (k, v) =>\n            s\"$nextIndent\\\"$k\\\": ${serializeToJson(v, depth + 1)}\"\n          }\n          s\"{$newline${pairs.mkString(\",\" + newline)}$newline$indent}\"\n        }\n      case seq: Seq[_] =>\n        if (seq.isEmpty) \"[]\"\n        else {\n          val items = seq.map(serializeToJson(_, depth + 1))\n          s\"[$newline$nextIndent${items.mkString(\",\" + newline + nextIndent)}$newline$indent]\"\n        }\n      case other => \"\\\"\" + escapeString(other.toString) + \"\\\"\"\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Escape special characters in JSON strings.\n    */\n  private def escapeString(s: String): String = {\n    s.replace(\"\\\\\", \"\\\\\\\\\")\n      .replace(\"\\\"\", \"\\\\\\\"\")\n      .replace(\"\\n\", \"\\\\n\")\n      .replace(\"\\r\", \"\\\\r\")\n      .replace(\"\\t\", \"\\\\t\")\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Extract and tokenize a value at a JSON path.\n    *\n    * Path format: \"key1.key2.0.key3\" (dot notation with array indices)\n    *\n    * @param value The JSON structure\n    * @param path The dot-notation path\n    * @return Some(Token) if path found, None otherwise\n    */\n  def tokenizePath(value: Any, path: String): Option[Token] = {\n    val parts = path.split(\"\\\\.\").toList\n    traversePath(value, parts).map(tokenize)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Traverse a JSON structure following a path.\n    *\n    * @param current Current value being traversed\n    * @param remaining Remaining path components\n    * @return Some(value) at path, None if path not found\n    */\n  private def traversePath(current: Any, remaining: List[String]): Option[Any] =\n    remaining match {\n      case Nil => Some(current)\n\n      case part :: rest =>\n        current match {\n part.forall(_.isDigit) =>\n            val idx = part.toInt\n            if (idx >= 0 && idx < seq.length)\n              traversePath(seq(idx), rest)\n            else\n              None\n\n ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer that splits text by whitespace with optional post-processing.\n  *\n  * This demonstrates practical tokenization for NLP and text processing.\n  * Uses immutable collections and functional transformations.\n  *\n  * @param lowercase Convert tokens to lowercase\n  * @param minLength Filter out tokens shorter than this\n  * @param maxLength Truncate tokens longer than this\n  * @param stripPunctuation Remove leading/trailing punctuation\n  */\nclass WhitespaceTokenizer(\n  lowercase: Boolean = false,\n  minLength: Int = 0,\n  maxLength: Option[Int] = None,\n  stripPunctuation: Boolean = false\n) {\n  require(minLength >= 0, \"minLength must be non-negative\")\n  require(maxLength.forall(_ > 0), \"maxLength must be positive\")\n\n  private val punctuation = Set(\".,!?;:'\\\"()[]{}\")\n\n  /**\n    * Process a single word into an optional token string.\n    *\n    * @param word The word to process\n    * @return Some(processed) if word is valid, None if filtered out\n    */\n  private def processToken(word: String): Option[String] = {\n    var result = word\n\n    if (stripPunctuation) {\n      result = result.filterNot(punctuation.contains(_))\n    }\n\n    if (lowercase) {\n      result = result.toLowerCase\n    }\n\n    if (result.length < minLength) {\n      None\n    } else if (maxLength.exists(result.length > _)) {\n      Some(result.take(maxLength.get))\n    } else if (result.nonEmpty) {\n      Some(result)\n    } else {\n      None\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Split text tokenType = TokenType.String,\n          metadata = Map(\"position\" -> idx, \"original\" -> word)\n        )\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Split text by whitespace and return list of token strings.\n    *\n    * Convenience method that returns just the string values.\n    *\n    * @param text Input text to tokenize\n    * @return List of token strings\n    */\n  def tokenizeToStrings(text: String): List[String] =\n    tokenize(text).map(_.value)\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize text and return tokens with character positions.\n    *\n    * @param text Input text to tokenize\n    * @return List of tuples (token_string, start_pos, end_pos)\n    */\n  def tokenizeWithPositions(text: String): List[(String, Int, Int)] = {\n    val words = text.split(\"\\\\s+\").toList\n    var currentPos = 0\n    val results = scala.collection.mutable.ListBuffer[(String, Int, Int)]()\n\n    for (word <- words) {\n      val start = text.indexOf(word, tokenize(text).length\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Builder Pattern with Fluent Interface\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Fluent builder for creating custom tokenizers.\n  *\n  * The builder pattern with method chaining is idiomatic def withMetadata(pairs: (String, Any)*): TokenizerBuilder[T] = {\n    _metadata = _metadata ++ pairs\n    this\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Build the final tokenizer function.\n    *\n    * @return A function that tokenizes values of type T\n    */\n  def build(): T => Token = { value =>\n    // Validate\n    for (validator <- _normalizers) {\n      if (!_validators.forall(_(value))) {\n        throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Validation failed for $value\")\n      }\n    }\n\n    // Convert to string\n    var strValue = value.toString\n\n    // Normalize\n    for (normalizer <- _normalizers) {\n      strValue = normalizer(strValue)\n    }\n\n    Token(strValue, TokenType.String, _metadata)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Build with Try wrapper for error handling.\n    *\n    * @return A function that {\n        strValue = normalizer(strValue)\n      }\n\n      Token(strValue, TokenType.String, _metadata)\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nobject TokenizerBuilder {\n  /**\n    * Create a new builder for the given    case Right(bd) =>\n        (String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", bd.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), \"BigDecimal\")\n    }\n\n    Token(strValue, TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> originalType))\n case bd: BigDecimal =>\n      Token(String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", bd.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"BigDecimal\"))\n\n    case other =>\n      throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Unsupported numeric type: ${other.getClass.getName}\")\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Temporal Tokenizer Implementation\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for date/time types.\n  *\n  * @param formatOption Optional custom format string\n  */\nclass TemporalTokenizer(formatOption: Option[String] = None) extends BaseTokenizer[Any] {\n\n  private val isoFormat = DateTimeFormatter.ISO_LOCAL_DATE_TIME\n  private val dateFormat = DateTimeFormatter.ISO_LOCAL_DATE\n\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case dt: LocalDateTime =>\n      val formatter = formatOption\n        .map(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern)\n        .getOrElse(isoFormat)\n      Token(dt.format(formatter), TokenType.Temporal)\n\n    case ld: LocalDate =>\n      val formatter = formatOption\n        .map(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern)\n ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer that handles multiple types with type-based dispatch.\n  *\n  * Instead of Python's duck typing, uses pattern matching for type-safe dispatch.\n  * This demonstrates Scala's preference for sealed hierarchies and exhaustive matching\n  * over runtime type checking.\n  */\nclass UniversalTokenizer {\n  private val stringTokenizer = new StringTokenizer()\n  private val numericTokenizer = new SimpleNumericTokenizer()\n  private val temporalTokenizer = new TemporalTokenizer()\n\n  /**\n    * Dispatch to appropriate tokenizer based on value type.\n    *\n    * Uses pattern matching for safe, exhaustive type handling.\n    * More idiomatic than Python's isinstance checks.\n    *\n    * @param value The value to tokenize\n    * @return Token representation of the value\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case null =>\n      Token(\"NULL\", TokenType.Null)\n\n    case s: String =>\n      stringTokenizer.tokenize(Left(s))\n\n    case b: Array[Byte] =>\n      stringTokenizer.tokenize(Right(b))\n\n    case i: Int =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(i)\n\n    case l: Long =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(l)\n\n    case d: Double =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(d)\n\n    case f: Float =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(f)\n\n    case bd: BigDecimal =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(bd)\n\n    case dt: LocalDateTime =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenize(dt)\n\n    case ld: LocalDate =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenize(ld)\n\n    case zdt: ZonedDateTime =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenize(zdt)\n\n    case t: Tokenizable =>\n      Token(t.toToken, TokenType.Structured)\n\n    case other =>\n      // Fallback: attempt string conversion with fallback marker\n      Token(other.toString, TokenType.String, Map(\"fallback\" -> true))\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Tokenizable Protocol (Structural Subtyping)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Trait representing anything that can be converted to a token string.\n  *\n  * Scala traits with single methods can be used as lightweight protocol types.\n  */\ntrait Tokenizable {\n  def toToken: String\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Token Registry with Nested Generics\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Registry with complex nested generic types and handler composition.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates proper handling of mutable state in Scala using\n  * immutable collections and functional patterns.\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of items stored in containers\n  */\nclass TokenRegistry[T] {\n  private var _registry: Map[String, TokenContainer[T]] = Map.empty\n  private var _handlers: Vector[T => Option[Token]] = Vector.empty\n\n  /**\n    * Register a container of items under a key.\n    */\n  def register(key: String, container: TokenContainer[T]): Unit = {\n    _registry = _registry.updated(key, container)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Add a handler function that attempts to tokenize an item.\n    *\n    * Handler returns Option[Token] to indicate success/failure.\n    */\n  def addHandler(handler: T => Option[Token]): Unit = {\n    _handlers = _handlers :+ handler\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Process all items in a container through all handlers.\n    *\n    * Uses first handler that returns Some(token), accumulates None for unhandled items.\n    *\n    * @param key The container key\n    * @return List of Option[Token] results\n    */\n  def process(key: String): List[Option[Token]] = {\n    _registry.get(key) match {\n      case None => List.empty\n\n      case Some(container) =>\n        container.getAll.map { item =>\n          _handlers.view\n            .map(_(item))\n            .find(_.isDefined)\n            .flatten\n        }.toList\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Functor and Monad Operations\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Generic functor wrapper for tokens with functional operations.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates functional programming patterns in Scala:\n  * - map for transformations\n  * - flatMap for chaining operations\n  * - getOrElse for null-safe extraction\n  *\n  * @tparam T The wrapped value type\n  */\ncase class TokenFunctor[T](value: T) {\n  /**\n    * Apply a function to the wrapped value.\n    */\n  def map[U](func: T => U): TokenFunctor[U] = TokenFunctor(func(value))\n\n  /**\n    * Chain operations that return TokenFunctor.\n    */\n  def flatMap[U](func: T => TokenFunctor[U]): pure/return constructor.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates monadic operations without case class inheritance\n  * (which is not allowed in Scala 2.13).\n  */\nclass TokenMonad[T](val value: T) extends TokenFunctor[T](value) {\n  /**\n    * Applicative apply operation.\n    */\n  def ap[U](funcMonad: TokenMonad[T => U]): TokenMonad[U] =\n    new TokenMonad(funcMonad.value(value))\n\n  override def equals(obj: Any): Boolean = obj match {\n    case other: TokenMonad[_] => value == other.value\n    case _ => false\n  }\n\n  override def hashCode(): Int = value.hashCode()\n\n  override def
