TokenMonad(value)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// JSON Structure Tokenization\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for JSON structures with recursive type support.\n  *\n  * In Scala, recursive types must be explicitly defined using case classes\n  * or sealed traits, unlike Python's recursive type aliases.\n  *\n  * Uses simple JSON serialization without external dependencies.\n  *\n  * @param pretty Whether to format JSON with indentation\n  */\nclass JsonTokenizer(pretty: Boolean = false) {\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a JSON value.\n    *\n    * @param value The JSON value (String, Int, Boolean, Map, Seq, or null)\n    * @return Token representing the JSON\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = {\n    val jsonStr = serializeToJson(value, 0)\n    Token(jsonStr, TokenType.Structured, Map(\"json\" -> true))\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Simple JSON serialization helper.\n    */\n  private def serializeToJson(value: Any, depth: Int): String = {\n    val indent = if (pretty) \"  \" * depth else \"\"\n    val nextIndent = if (pretty) \"  \" * (depth + 1) else \"\"\n    val newline = if (pretty) \"\\n\" else \"\"\n\n    value match {\n      case null => \"null\"\n      case b: Boolean => b.toString\n      case n: Number => n.toString\n      case s: String => \"\\\"\" + escapeString(s) + \"\\\"\"\n      case map: Map[_, _] =>\n        if (map.isEmpty) \"{}\"\n        else {\n          val pairs = map.asInstanceOf[Map[String, Any]].map { case (k, v) =>\n            s\"$nextIndent\\\"$k\\\": ${serializeToJson(v, depth + 1)}\"\n          }\n          s\"{$newline${pairs.mkString(\",\" + newline)}$newline$indent}\"\n        }\n      case seq: Seq[_] =>\n        if (seq.isEmpty) \"[]\"\n        else {\n          val items = seq.map(serializeToJson(_, depth + 1))\n          s\"[$newline$nextIndent${items.mkString(\",\" + newline + nextIndent)}$newline$indent]\"\n        }\n      case other => \"\\\"\" + escapeString(other.toString) + \"\\\"\"\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    *    */\n  def tokenizePath(value: Any, path: String): Option[Token] = {\n    val parts = path.split(\"\\\\.\").toList\n    traversePath(value, parts).map(tokenize)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Traverse a JSON */\n  private def traversePath(current: Any, remaining: List[String]): Option[Any] =\n    remaining match {\n      case Nil => Some(current)\n\n      case part :: rest =>\n        current match {\n          case map: Map[_, _] =>\n            map.asInstanceOf[Map[String, Any]]\n              .get(part)\n              .flatMap(traversePath(_, rest))\n\n          case seq: Seq[_] if part.forall(_.isDigit) =>\n            val idx = part.toInt\n            if (idx >= 0 && idx < seq.length)\n              traversePath(seq(idx), rest)\n            else\n              None\n\n          case _ => None\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Whitespace Tokenizer - Basic Text Tokenization\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer that splits text by whitespace with optional post-processing.\n  *\n  * This demonstrates practical tokenization for NLP and text processing.\n  * Uses immutable collections and functional transformations.\n  *\n  * @param lowercase Convert tokens to lowercase\n  * @param minLength Filter out tokens _received = _received :+ item\n  }\n\n  def drain(): Vector[Any] = {\n    val result = _received\n    _received = Vector.empty\n    result\n  }\n\n  def size: Int = _received.length\n}\n\n/**\n  * Invariant handler - requires exact type matching.\n  * T without variance modifier means invariant: neither covariant nor contravariant.\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of value to handle (invariant)\n  */\nclass BivariantHandler[T](initialValue: T) {\n  private var _value: T = initialValue\n\n  def get: T = _value\n\n  def set(value: T): Unit = {\n    _value = value\n  }\n\n  def transform(func: T => T): T = {\n    _value = func(_value)\n    _value\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Base Tokenizer Trait\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Abstract trait for tokenizers with generic input type.\n  *\n  * In Scala, we use traits instead of abstract base classes for:\n  * - Better composition (can mix multiple traits)\n  * - Linearization of method resolution\n  * - Interface definition without state\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of values this tokenizer accepts\n  */\ntrait BaseTokenizer[T] {\n  /**\n    * Convert a single value to a token.\n    *\n    * @param value The value to tokenize\n    * @return A Token representing the value\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: T): Token\n\n  /**\n    * Lazily tokenize multiple values using a Stream (lazy sequence).\n    *\n    * @param values The values to tokenize\n    * @return A LazyList of tokens (evaluated on demand)\n    */\n  def tokenizeBatch(values: Iterable[T]): LazyList[Token] =\n    LazyList.from(values).map(tokenize)\n\n  /**\n    * Convert a value to a token, wrapping in Try for error handling.\n    *\n    * @param value The value to tokenize\n    * @return Try[Token] with success or failure\n    */\n  def toToken(value: T): Try[Token] = Try(tokenize(value))\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// String Tokenizer Implementation\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for string and byte array types.\n  *\n  * Handles encoding/decoding and optional normalization.\n  * Uses sealed trait to restrict T to String or Array[Byte].\n  *\n  * @param encoding Character encoding (default UTF-8)\n  * @param normalizer Optional function to normalize strings\n  */\nclass StringTokenizer(\n  encoding: String = \"UTF-8\",\n  normalizer: String => String = identity\n) extends BaseTokenizer[Either[String, Array[Byte]]] {\n\n  def tokenize(value: Either[String, Array[Byte]]): Token = {\n    val strValue = value match {\n      case Left(s) => s\n      case Right(bytes) => new String(bytes, encoding)\n    }\n\n    val normalized = normalizer(strValue)\n    Token(normalized, TokenType.String)\n  }\n}\n\nobject StringTokenizer {\n  /**\n    * Create a StringTokenizer with a normalizer that converts to lowercase.\n    */\n  def lowercaseNormalizer: StringTokenizer =\n    new StringTokenizer(normalizer = _.toLowerCase)\n\n  /**\n    * Create a StringTokenizer with a normalizer that trims whitespace.\n    */\n  def trimNormalizer: StringTokenizer =\n    new StringTokenizer(normalizer = _.trim)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Numeric Tokenizer Implementation\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for numeric types with precision handling.\n  *\n  * Uses a sealed trait to restrict T to Int, Long, Double, Float, or BigDecimal.\n  *\n  * @param precision Number of decimal places for floating point values\n  * @param formatOptions Additional formatting options\n  */\nclass NumericTokenizer(\n  precision: Int = 6,\n  formatOptions: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n) extends BaseTokenizer[Either[Either[Either[Int, Long], Either[Double, Float]], BigDecimal]] {\n\n  require(precision >= 0, \"Precision must be non-negative\")\n\n  def tokenize(value: Either[Either[Either[Int, Long], Either[Double, Float]], BigDecimal]): Token = {\n    val (strValue, originalType) = value match {\n      case Left(Left(Left(i))) =>\n        (i.toString, \"Int\")\n\n      case Left(Left(Right(l))) =>\n        (l.toString, \"Long\")\n\n      case Left(Right(Left(d))) =>\n        (String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", d.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), \"Double\")\n\n      case Left(Right(Right(f))) =>\n        (String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", f.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), \"Float\")\n\n      case Right(bd) =>\n        (String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", bd.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), \"BigDecimal\")\n    }\n\n    Token(strValue, TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> originalType))\n  }\n}\n\n// Simplified numeric tokenizer using pattern matching\nclass SimpleNumericTokenizer(precision: Int = 6) extends BaseTokenizer[Any] {\n  require(precision >= 0, \"Precision must be non-negative\")\n\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case i: Int =>\n      Token(i.toString, TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"Int\"))\n\n    case l: Long =>\n      Token(l.toString, TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"Long\"))\n\n    case d: Double =>\n      Token(String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", d.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"Double\"))\n\n    case f: Float =>\n      Token(String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", f.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"Float\"))\n\n    case bd: BigDecimal =>\n      Token(String.format(s\"%.${precision}f\", bd.asInstanceOf[AnyRef]), TokenType.Numeric, Map(\"originalType\" -> \"BigDecimal\"))\n\n    case other =>\n      throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Unsupported numeric type: ${other.getClass.getName}\")\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Temporal Tokenizer Implementation\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for date/time types.\n  *\n  * @param formatOption Optional custom format string\n  */\nclass TemporalTokenizer(formatOption: Option[String] = None) extends BaseTokenizer[Any] {\n\n  private val isoFormat = DateTimeFormatter.ISO_LOCAL_DATE_TIME\n  private val dateFormat = DateTimeFormatter.ISO_LOCAL_DATE\n\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case dt: LocalDateTime =>\n      val formatter = formatOption\n        .map(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern)\n        .getOrElse(isoFormat)\n      Token(dt.format(formatter), TokenType.Temporal)\n\n    case ld: LocalDate =>\n      val formatter = formatOption\n        .map(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern)\n        .getOrElse(dateFormat)\n      Token(ld.format(formatter), TokenType.Temporal)\n\n    case zdt: ZonedDateTime =>\n      val formatter = formatOption\n        .map(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern)\n        .getOrElse(isoFormat)\n      Token(zdt.format(formatter), TokenType.Temporal)\n\n    case other =>\n      throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Unsupported temporal type: ${other.getClass.getName}\")\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Universal Tokenizer (Polymorphic Dispatch)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer that handles multiple types with type-based dispatch.\n  *\n  * Instead of Python's duck typing, uses pattern matching for type-safe dispatch.\n  * This demonstrates Scala's preference for sealed hierarchies and exhaustive matching\n  * over runtime type checking.\n  */\nclass UniversalTokenizer {\n  private val stringTokenizer = new StringTokenizer()\n  private val numericTokenizer = new SimpleNumericTokenizer()\n  private val temporalTokenizer = new TemporalTokenizer()\n\n  /**\n    * Dispatch to appropriate tokenizer based on value type.\n    *\n    * Uses pattern matching for safe, exhaustive type handling.\n    * More idiomatic than Python's isinstance checks.\n    *\n    * @param value The value to tokenize\n    * @return Token representation of the value\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case null =>\n      Token(\"NULL\", TokenType.Null)\n\n    case s: String =>\n      stringTokenizer.tokenize(Left(s))\n\n    case b: Array[Byte] =>\n      stringTokenizer.tokenize(Right(b))\n\n    case i: Int =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(i)\n\n    case l: Long =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(l)\n\n    case d: Double =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(d)\n\n    case f: Float =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(f)\n\n    case bd: BigDecimal =>\n      numericTokenizer.tokenize(bd)\n\n    case dt: LocalDateTime =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenize(dt)\n\n    case ld: LocalDate =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenize(ld)\n\n    case zdt: ZonedDateTime =>\n      temporalTokenizer.tokenize(zdt)\n\n    case t: Tokenizable =>\n      Token(t.toToken, TokenType.Structured)\n\n    case other =>\n      // Fallback: attempt string conversion with fallback marker\n      Token(other.toString, TokenType.String, Map(\"fallback\" -> true))\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Tokenizable Protocol (Structural Subtyping)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Trait representing anything that can be converted to a token string.\n  *\n  * Scala traits with single methods can be used as lightweight protocol types.\n  */\ntrait Tokenizable {\n  def toToken: String\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Token Registry with Nested Generics\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Registry with complex nested generic types and handler composition.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates proper handling of mutable state in Scala using\n  * immutable collections and functional patterns.\n  *\n  * @tparam T The type of items stored in containers\n  */\nclass TokenRegistry[T] {\n  private var _registry: Map[String, TokenContainer[T]] = Map.empty\n  private var _handlers: Vector[T => Option[Token]] = Vector.empty\n\n  /**\n    * Register a container of items under a key.\n    */\n  def register(key: String, container: TokenContainer[T]): Unit = {\n    _registry = _registry.updated(key, container)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Add a handler function that attempts to tokenize an item.\n    *\n    * Handler returns Option[Token] to indicate success/failure.\n    */\n  def addHandler(handler: T => Option[Token]): Unit = {\n    _handlers = _handlers :+ handler\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Process all items in a container through all handlers.\n    *\n    * Uses first handler that returns Some(token), accumulates None for unhandled items.\n    *\n    * @param key The container key\n    * @return List of Option[Token] results\n    */\n  def process(key: String): List[Option[Token]] = {\n    _registry.get(key) match {\n      case None => List.empty\n\n      case Some(container) =>\n        container.getAll.map { item =>\n          _handlers.view\n            .map(_(item))\n            .find(_.isDefined)\n            .flatten\n        }.toList\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Functor and Monad Operations\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Generic functor wrapper for tokens with functional operations.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates functional programming patterns in Scala:\n  * - map for transformations\n  * - flatMap for chaining operations\n  * - getOrElse for null-safe extraction\n  *\n  * @tparam T The wrapped value type\n  */\ncase class TokenFunctor[T](value: T) {\n  /**\n    * Apply a function to the wrapped value.\n    */\n  def map[U](func: T => U): TokenFunctor[U] = TokenFunctor(func(value))\n\n  /**\n    * Chain operations that return TokenFunctor.\n    */\n  def flatMap[U](func: T => TokenFunctor[U]): equals(obj: Any): Boolean = obj match {\n    case other: TokenMonad[_] => value == other.value\n    case _ => false\n  }\n\n  override def hashCode(): Int = value.hashCode()\n\n  override def TokenMonad(value)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// JSON Structure Tokenization\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for JSON structures with recursive type support.\n  *\n  * In Scala, recursive types must be explicitly defined using case classes\n  * or sealed traits, unlike Python's recursive type aliases.\n  *\n  * Uses simple JSON serialization without external dependencies.\n  *\n  * @param pretty Whether to format JSON with indentation\n  */\nclass JsonTokenizer(pretty: Boolean = false) {\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a JSON value.\n    *\n    * @param value The JSON value (String, Int, Boolean, Map, Seq, or null)\n    * @return Token representing the JSON\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = {\n    val jsonStr = serializeToJson(value, 0)\n    Token(jsonStr, TokenType.Structured, Map(\"json\" -> true))\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Simple JSON serialization helper.\n    */\n  private def serializeToJson(value: Any, depth: Int): String = {\n    val indent = if (pretty) \"  \" * depth else \"\"\n    val nextIndent = if (pretty) \"  \" * (depth + 1) else \"\"\n    val newline = if (pretty) \"\\n\" else \"\"\n\n    value match {\n      case null => \"null\"\n      case b: Boolean => b.toString\n      case n: Number => n.toString\n      case s: String => \"\\\"\" + escapeString(s) + \"\\\"\"\n      case map: Map[_, _] =>\n        if (map.isEmpty) \"{}\"\n        else {\n          val pairs = map.asInstanceOf[Map[String, Any]].map { case (k, v) =>\n            s\"$nextIndent\\\"$k\\\": ${serializeToJson(v, depth + 1)}\"\n          }\n          s\"{$newline${pairs.mkString(\",\" + newline)}$newline$indent}\"\n        }\n      case seq: Seq[_] =>\n        if (seq.isEmpty) \"[]\"\n        else {\n          val items = seq.map(serializeToJson(_, depth + 1))\n          s\"[$newline$nextIndent${items.mkString(\",\" + newline + nextIndent)}$newline$indent]\"\n        }\n      case other => \"\\\"\" + escapeString(other.toString) + \"\\\"\"\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * structure following a path.\n    *\n    * @param current Current value being traversed\n    * @param remaining Remaining path components\n    * @return Some(value) at path, None if path not found\n    */\n  private def traversePath(current: Any, remaining: List[String]): Option[Any] =\n    remaining match {\n      case Nil => Some(current)\n\n      case part :: rest =>\n        current match {\n          case map: Map[_, _] =>\n            map.asInstanceOf[Map[String, Any]]\n              .get(part)\n              .flatMap(traversePath(_, rest))\n\n          case seq: Seq[_] if part.forall(_.isDigit) =>\n            val idx = part.toInt\n            if (idx >= 0 && idx < seq.length)\n              traversePath(seq(idx), rest)\n            else\n              None\n\n ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer that splits text by whitespace with optional post-processing.\n  *\n  * This demonstrates practical tokenization for NLP and text processing.\n  * Uses immutable collections and functional transformations.\n  *\n  * @param lowercase Convert tokens to lowercase\n  * @param minLength Filter out tokens false,\n  minLength: Int = 0,\n  maxLength: Option[Int] = None,\n  stripPunctuation: Boolean = false\n) {\n  require(minLength >= 0, \"minLength must be non-negative\")\n  require(maxLength.forall(_ > 0), \"maxLength must be positive\")\n\n  private val punctuation = Set(\".,!?;:'\\\"()[]{}\")\n\n  /**\n    * Process a single word into an optional token string.\n    *\n    * @param word The word to process\n    * @return Some(processed) if word is valid, None if filtered out\n    */\n  private def processToken(word: String): Option[String] = {\n    var result = word\n\n    if (stripPunctuation)    } else if (maxLength.exists(result.length > _)) {\n      Some(result.take(maxLength.get))\n    } else if (result.nonEmpty) {\n      Some(result)\n    } else {\n      None\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n text.split(\"\\\\s+\").toList\n    var currentPos = 0\n    val results = scala.collection.mutable.ListBuffer[(String, Int, Int)]()\n\n    for (word <- words) {\n      val start = text.indexOf(word, results.toList\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Return the number of tokens in the text.\n    *\n    * @param text Input text\n    * @return Token count\n    */\n  def countTokens(text: String): Int = ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Fluent builder for creating custom tokenizers.\n  *\n  * The builder pattern with method chaining is idiomatic that tokenizes values of type T\n    */\n  def build(): T => Token = { value =>\n    // Validate\n    for (validator <- _normalizers) {\n      if (!_validators.forall(_(value))) {\n        throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Validation failed for $value\")\n      }\n    }\n\n    // Convert to string\n    var strValue = value.toString\n\n    // Normalize\n    for (normalizer <- _normalizers) {\n      strValue = normalizer(strValue)\n    }\n\n    Token(strValue, TokenType.String, _metadata)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Build with Try wrapper for error handling.\n    *\n    * @return A function that safely tokenizes values\n    */\n  def buildSafe(): T => Try[Token] = { value =>\n    Try {\n      // Validate\n      if (!_validators.forall(_(value))) {\n        throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Validation failed for $value\")\n      }\n\n      // Convert to string\n      var strValue = value.toString\n\n      // Normalize\n      for (normalizer <- _normalizers) {\n        strValue = normalizer(strValue)\n      }\n\n      Token(strValue, TokenType.String, _metadata)\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nobject TokenizerBuilder {\n  /**\n    * Create a new builder for the given The Scala implementation maintains all functionality while following Scala conventions, best practices, and leveraging the language's type system and functional programming capabilities.\n\n## File Location\n- **Scala Source**: `/root/Tokenizer.scala` (939 lines)\n- **Test File**: `/root/TokenizerTest.scala` (test demonstrations)\n- **Compilation Status**: ✅ Successfully compiles with Scala 2.13.12\n\n## Key Translation Decisions\n\n### 1. **TokenType: Sealed Trait Instead of Enum**\n```python\n# Python\nclass TokenType(Enum):\n    STRING = \"string\"\n    NUMERIC = \"numeric\"\n```\n\n```scala\n// Scala - Sealed trait for better extensibility\nsealed trait TokenType {\n  def name: String\n}\n\nobject TokenType {\n  case object String extends TokenType { val name = \"string\" }\n  case object Numeric extends TokenType { val name = \"numeric\" }\n  // ...\n}\n```\n\n**Rationale**: Sealed traits provide exhaustive pattern matching at compile time and are more idiomatic in Scala. The nested case objects are singletons by nature.\n\n### 2. **Token: Case Class Instead of Frozen Dataclass**\n```python\n# Python\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Token:\n    value: str\n    token_type: TokenType\n    metadata: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)\n```\n\n```scala\n// Scala\ncase class Token(\n  value: String,\n  tokenType: TokenType,\n  metadata: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n)\n```\n\n**Rationale**: Case classes automatically provide:\n- Value equality semantics\n- Immutability by default\n- Auto-generated `equals`, `hashCode`, `toString`\n- Pattern matching support\n- Copy constructor via `.copy()` method\n\n### 3. **BaseTokenizer: Trait Instead of ABC**\n```python\n# Python\nclass BaseTokenizer(ABC, Generic[T]):\n    @abstractmethod\n    def tokenize(self, value: T) -> Token:\n        pass\n\n    def tokenize_batch(self, values: Iterable[T]) -> Iterator[Token]:\n        for v in values:\n            yield self.tokenize(v)\n```\n\n```scala\n// Scala\ntrait BaseTokenizer[T] {\n  def tokenize(value: T): Token\n\n  def tokenizeBatch(values: Iterable[T]): LazyList[Token] =\n    LazyList.from(values).map(tokenize)\n}\n```\n\n**Rationale**:\n- Traits are Scala's preferred approach for interfaces and mixins\n- `LazyList` provides lazy evaluation (like Python generators)\n- Method implementations in traits are idiomatic\n\n### 4. **Option[T] Instead of None/null**\n```python\n# Python\ndef tokenizePath(self, value: JsonValue, path: str) -> Token | None:\n    # ... may return None\n    return None\n```\n\n```scala\n// Scala\ndef tokenizePath(value: Any, path: String): Option[Token] = {\n  // ... Option[Token] is more idiomatic\n  traversePath(value, parts).map(tokenize)\n}\n```\n\n**Rationale**: Scala avoids null by design. `Option[T]` (Some/None) is composable and works well with functional transformations.\n\n### 5. **Pattern Matching Instead of isinstance()**\n```python\n# Python\nif isinstance(value, str):\n    return self._string_tokenizer.tokenize(value)\nelif isinstance(value, (int, float, Decimal)):\n    return self._numeric_tokenizer.tokenize(value)\n```\n\n```scala\n// Scala\nvalue match {\n  case s: String => stringTokenizer.tokenize(Left(s))\n  case i: Int => numericTokenizer.tokenize(i)\n  case l: Long => numericTokenizer.tokenize(l)\n  case d: Double => numericTokenizer.tokenize(d)\n  case other => Token(other.toString, TokenType.String, Map(\"fallback\" -> true))\n}\n```\n\n**Rationale**: Pattern matching is:\n- Type-safe and benefits from compiler exhaustiveness checking\n- More readable and expressive\n- The idiomatic Scala way instead of runtime type checking\n\n### 6. **Immutable Collections by Default**\n```python\n# Python - mutable\nself._tokens: list[Token] = []\nself._tokens.append(token)\n```\n\n```scala\n// Scala - immutable by default\nprivate var _tokens: Vector[Token] = Vector.empty\n_tokens = _tokens :+ token  // Creates new Vector\n\n// Or use functional approach:\ntokens.filter(_.active).map(_.value)\n```\n\n**Rationale**: Immutable collections are:\n- Safer in concurrent/distributed systems\n- Enable equational reasoning about code\n- More efficient with structural sharing\n\n### 7. **Try for Error Handling**\n```python\n# Python\ntry:\n    # tokenize\nexcept ValueError:\n    # handle error\n```\n\n```scala\n// Scala\ndef toToken(value: T): Try[Token] = Try(tokenize(value))\n\n// Usage\nval result = toToken(value) match {\n  case Success(token) => // handle success\n  case Failure(ex) => // handle failure\n}\n```\n\n**Rationale**: `Try[T]` is composable with other Scala types and supports functional error handling.\n\n### 8. **Named Parameters for Clarity**\n```python\n# Python\nWhitespaceTokenizer(lowercase=True, min_length=0, max_length=10)\n```\n\n```scala\n// Scala - same approach works\nnew WhitespaceTokenizer(\n  lowercase = true,\n  minLength = 0,\n  maxLength = Some(10)\n)\n```\n\n**Rationale**: Scala supports named parameters; they improve readability for complex constructor calls.\n\n## Complete Class and Function Mapping\n\n| Python | Scala | Notes |\n|--------|-------|-------|\n| `TokenType` (Enum) | `sealed trait TokenType` | Sealed trait with case objects |\n| `Token` (dataclass) | `case class Token` | Case class for immutability |\n| `MutableTokenBatch` | `TokenBatch` class | Demonstrates mutable state when needed |\n| `TokenContainer` | `TokenContainer[+T]` | Covariant generic container |\n| `TokenSink` | `TokenSink[-T]` | Contravariant generic sink |\n| `BivariantHandler` | `BivariantHandler[T]` | Invariant generic handler |\n| `BaseTokenizer` | `trait BaseTokenizer[T]` | Abstract trait with default impl |\n| `StringTokenizer` | `class StringTokenizer` | Handles Either[String, Array[Byte]] |\n| hashCode, toString, and copy\n3. **Immutability**: Prefer `val` and immutable collections (Vector, Map, List)\n4. **Option/Either**: Replace null with Option[T] and Either[L, R]\n5. **Pattern
